# Isolated publication writer

Read your complete execution plan and its entire lossless reader, including every shared block, in bounded untruncated outputs. Only the plan and reader are allowed evidence. Do not inspect peers, sessions, credentials, other debates or environment; do not browse, make paid calls or spawn agents. Report complete reading and the sole absolute output, then wait for controller authenticated reading release before authoring. Use the frozen source, resolved judgments, calculated scores and skeleton. Preserve identity, motion, scope, section/card order, times, numbers and assessment substance. Reference profiles provide a comparison corpus, never debate evidence or reusable prose.

Output exactly {summary,quotes,moves,overall,logicalExtension,noveltyMap,editorialReview}. Do not output another assembled candidate. The checker inserts these leaves into the immutable skeleton.

- summary: 8–35 words describing the assessed exchange and its result fairly.
- quotes: {pro:{text,context},con:{text,context}}. Text is a verbatim frozen quoteEligibleExactSpans string of that side, 3–18 words; preserve source case and punctuation. Read each without its context: it must express a complete thought, retaining any meaning-changing qualification. Contexts are 12–55 words; they explain use, not complete a fragment. Prefer clearly articulated claims over pronoun-dependent snippets or biographical remarks.
- moves: exactly 22 entries in skeleton publication order (section, exchange, pro then con), each {moveId,words,critique}. Description normally 22–28 words: actual claim, support/comparison and implication, without padding or invented premises. Mean at least20.9; no more than25% below20words. No tags yet.
- Each critique has exactly four sentences, beginning Strongest feature:, Principal limitation:, Live burden:, Locked score:, in that order. 105–130 words AND at least880 characters; target112–122 substantive words while satisfying both gates. The last sentence states the exact card score as N/100 with a specific explanation. Every sentence ends with terminal punctuation; avoid internal full stops in abbreviations. Discuss the actual inference, evidence, limitation and remaining burden. Do not turn withholding assent into an obligation to prove the contrary. Write source-specific prose manually, without interchangeable sentence-bank bodies, padded suffixes or repeated templates. Common six-word sequences present in at least one-quarter of critiques may cover at most15% of words on average and25% in any critique.
- overall: {pro:{strengths:[...],blunders:[{text,links:[]},...]},con:...}. At least3 concrete strengths and2 material blunders each, normally18–35words per item. Remain score-consistent; no invented claims, new numerical penalties or speculative labels. Links remain empty until separate rhetorical review.
- logicalExtension: {pro:{finalArgument:{thesis,premises,conclusion},newArguments:[{title,text},...]},con:...}. Thesis at least12words; four to six premises each at least12words; conclusion at least15words. Two to four distinct reinforcing arguments per side, title2–8words, text45–130words, preferably80–110 substantive words. The whole section is visibly labeled AI Contribution. Within it write direct philosophical prose, without repeated provenance or generic model/constraint framing. Strengthen both sides proportionately, distinguish new reasoning from recorded contributions, and preserve the question and opponent burden. Each reinforcing argument needs a genuinely new inference beyond the inventory. Preserve natural punctuation. Compare both sides' openings, sentence structures and conclusions for mirrored scaffolds and noun-swappable prose; compare the supplied independent corpus without recycling it.
- noveltyMap: one {side,title,closestMoveIds:[...],distinctInferentialContribution} for every reinforcing argument. Identify a concrete inferential addition, not a generic novelty assertion.
- editorialReview: {sourceFidelity,completeQuotes,scoreConsistency,nonformulaicCritiques,aiContributionNonformulaic,aiPunctuation,referenceComparison}. Each is a source-specific string at least40characters describing a review actually performed.

## Frozen source constraints

# Frozen source and assessment restrictions

Motion: Is intelligent design a legitimate scientific explanation for biological origins?

Scored sides: Stephen Meyer (pro); Peter Atkins (con).

By explicit editorial approval, this assessment scores only Stephen Meyer and Peter Atkins in their substantive discussion and both closing remarks, approximately 04:47–1:01:33, excluding programme breaks. This radio discussion has no timed formal rounds and also includes recurring advocate Mark Haville. Haville and host Justin Brierley remain unscored context: their arguments and quoted material are not credited to either scored participant, but Meyer and Atkins’s own replies remain eligible. Unequal prompting and opportunities to respond are considered. Film excerpts, introductions, promotions, Haville’s final promotional remarks, listener feedback and the outro supply no credit, penalty, inferred concession or failure-to-reply inference. The source uses automatic captions and supplementary automated audio checks where available; no direct human listening is claimed.

Event labels E0001–E2090 are one-based; all startEvent/endEvent fields are zero-based. Caption durations overlap speaker turns; a mixed event may contain an unscored tail, never words assignable by proximity alone.

1. The exact motion is Is intelligent design a legitimate scientific explanation for biological origins? Meyer defends a positive inference to intelligence and its scientific legitimacy. Atkins challenges its warrant and status. A critic does not need a completed origin-of-life account merely to question design, although any stronger positive naturalistic claim he actually makes remains assessable.

2. The editor explicitly approved Meyer and Atkins only. Mark Haville is a recurring substantive advocate, not Meyer’s team partner in this assessment. Justin Brierley contributes arguments as well as questions. Both remain context only; their claims, analogies, quotations, demands and rhetoric cannot be credited to either side. Primary speakers’ own replies and explicitly adopted reasoning remain eligible. Consider unequal prompting and opportunities to answer.

3. Assess the four retained programme blocks, including both primary closings. Film trailers and other prerecorded clips, introductions, promotional breaks, Haville’s promotional closing, listener feedback and the outro supply no credit, penalty, inferred concession or missing-reply inference. Full-source access is for scope audit and context, not permission to use excluded evidence.

4. Automatic captions contain overlapping events, mixed turns, misspelled names and corrupted short interjections. Treat disputed scientific and institutional claims as claims made in this debate, not independently verified facts. Do not import external research to repair or refute a speaker’s case, and never turn a recognition defect into a reasoning penalty.

5. Meyer’s opening invokes known causes of information and a historical method he attributes to Darwin. He differentiates functionally specified digital sequence from recurring physical order, allowing energy-driven vortices and other regular structures. Atkins invokes spontaneous structure, thermodynamics and evolution. Evaluate the distinctions actually defended rather than treating complexity, regular order and functional information as interchangeable by fiat.

6. Meyer initially allows change over time while questioning whether chance, law or their combination explain specified sequences. Later he explicitly accepts evolutionary biology as empirical and a valid historical science (about 42:50–43:22). Haville’s preceding denial that macroevolution is empirical belongs to Haville alone. Meyer’s agreement with Atkins must be preserved.

7. Meyer distinguishes information required before the first living/reproducing system from mutation and selection in existing organisms (about 49:13–50:14). He allows modest new information after life exists but questions large transitions. Haville’s preceding absolute assertion that new information cannot be added is not Meyer’s position. Do not collapse origin of life into biological evolution or invent a denial of all mutation-driven information change.

8. Atkins concedes that intelligent-design advocates ask questions scientists should answer, and acknowledges that current science does not yet explain everything. His simplicity/Occam argument is partly methodological and conditional. Do not transform either concession into acceptance of intelligent design, or treat his stated confidence in natural explanation as an already supplied detailed chemical mechanism.

9. On Richard Sternberg, Meyer corrects Brierley’s claim that Sternberg lost his job: Meyer describes removal of office, keys and access, scrutiny and demotion. He invokes peer review and an official report. The film and host’s stronger assertions are not his evidence independently established by this assessment. Preserve his correction and distinguish alleged institutional treatment from truth of design.

10. The Rosetta Stone causal analogy at about 27:40–28:34 is Meyer’s, despite the host initially inviting Mark. Atkins answers that mind is an activity of a material brain; Meyer calls that a materialist presupposition. Do not import Haville’s subsequent social-danger argument or his secondhand quotation about everything from nothing into either scored argument.

11. Meyer distinguishes ordinary operations of nature from questions about origins in response to Atkins’s intelligent-falling analogy. Atkins answers with the interconnectedness of scientific explanations and a preference for simple universal laws. Preserve both the intended domain distinction and the actual coherence challenge; neither alone demonstrates its conclusion by assertion.

12. Meyer contrasts a biological-data inference with biblical deduction. Atkins calls the approach covert creationism and intellectually lazy, identifying divine agency as a premature stopping point. Score the inferential content and actual representation, not tone or politeness alone. Meyer calls God a likely candidate for the inferred agent, not a uniquely established identification.

13. Meyer counters the science-stopper objection through a research agenda, historical religious motivations and the noncoding-DNA prediction; these are distinct kinds of support. Atkins concedes historical motivation but argues science has moved on, and says non-design scientists were already investigating noncoding DNA. Do not assume the claimed prediction was unique, verified or discriminating beyond the reasons actually presented.

14. Atkins’s final remarks defend continuing intelligible explanation and object to a purported limit on understanding; Meyer’s final remarks emphasize freedom of inquiry and room to debate scientific definitions. Atkins speaks after Meyer’s closing, so Meyer’s lack of a later answer is not a concession or automatic response failure.

15. Featured quotes must be exact complete standalone thoughts of the assigned primary speaker, 3–18 words, with necessary qualification preserved. Do not fix caption wording, concatenate separated excerpts, quote mixed-speaker fragments, or shorten a sentence before its meaningful conclusion.

16. The failed evolution-definitions audio input returned no transcription and has not been retried. No independent audio verification is claimed for that interval. Short unresolved overlapping questions around 17:33–18:00 may be context only; they cannot establish a scored claim, concession or contradiction. The underlying information-origin challenge is stated in clear primary-speaker turns elsewhere and need not be inferred from those interruptions.

17. The automated checks used valid primary-speaker references and a Haville reference tied to clear uninterrupted turns. The supplied Justin Brierley reference was subsequently identified as a film-trailer interval and is invalid: disregard all labels based on that reference. Host turns are identified only through named handoffs, programme-management language and dialogue continuity; an unmatched A/B label is not a new participant. No primary ownership may rest solely on an automated label. Preserve this limitation and require below-high confidence wherever material uncertainty remains.

18. The supplemental recognition at about 45:20–45:22 inserts short overlapping words resembling agreement from Atkins, while canonical captions are unclear. Do not infer that Atkins concedes the positive design argument is valid or not an argument from ignorance from this interjection. His sustained questions and objections provide the assessable position.

## Supplementary verification findings

- materialism-and-minds (1590–1817 seconds): The host’s proposed materialist-prejudice symmetry is context. Atkins explicitly acknowledges incomplete present explanations while invoking simplicity. Meyer, despite Mark being invited, makes the Rosetta Stone analogy; Atkins locates mind in a material brain. Haville alone develops the following social-danger and everything-from-nothing rhetoric. Do not use recognition-corrupted short overlap about mind as a concession.

- empirical-and-laziness (2475–2770 seconds): Haville challenges macroevolution as nonempirical; Atkins cites paleobiological evidence. Meyer explicitly agrees that evolutionary biology is empirical, then invokes historical scientific reasoning for design. Atkins’s inquiry-stopper/laziness challenge is his own, while the host’s formulation remains context. Haville makes the atheistic-gaps interjection. Meyer defends a positive known-cause inference and calls God a likely candidate. A tiny overlapping apparent Atkins agreement in supplemental recognition is not reliable concession evidence.

- information-and-origin (2805–3050 seconds): Haville raises Max Planck; Meyer separately adds Maxwell, but the general speech about modern religious scientists is Haville’s. Haville states that adding new information cannot happen; Atkins answers with copying mistakes, survival and propagation. Meyer then distinguishes origins before life from changes after life, allows modest information gains by mutation, questions major transitions, and says he would accept a demonstrated natural origin-of-life explanation. Preserve those qualifications and do not attribute Haville’s absolute claim to Meyer.

- inquiry-and-predictions (3050–3295 seconds): The host challenges laziness first; Meyer asks about assuming material processes regardless of evidence. Atkins distinguishes simple underlying ideas from complex outcomes and explicitly says design is unnecessary. Meyer advances the research/prediction example about noncoding DNA. Atkins answers that non-design scientists already investigated it. Haville asks that other researchers be allowed to answer; Atkins supplies the schoolyard dismissal. Only the primary speakers’ own arguments are eligible.

- both-primary-closings (3470–3697 seconds): Both closings are present. Meyer emphasizes freedom of inquiry, proper credentials and debate about scientific definitions; Atkins emphasizes no demonstrated need for design, premature agency answers and continuing human understanding. The host’s intervening framing is context. Atkins ends last; no later Meyer response opportunity exists.

No scoring evidence may depend on the failed first check, the invalid host reference, an uncertain tiny interjection, or a third participant’s words. If a selected material uncertainty still remains, report below-high confidence and request the required pre-adjudication verification; do not conceal uncertainty to avoid the gate.

Additional source-review clarification: missing explanation in Atkins’s brain/material claim concerns the inferential consequence of his clearly attributed words, never caption overlap or recognition quality. His closing is constructive; criticisms address his actual positive no-need claims and representation of Meyer, without demanding a completed origin theory merely to criticize design.

Use noninteractive shell transport with tty:false and safe single-quote escaping; never create a disk draft or use interactive PTY pasting. Ordinary unsaved mechanical corrections remain within this same context without extra permission. Hold the exact checked bytes with the maintained runner and keep its process alive. The controller inspects every field and alone releases the exact first save. An existing hold must not be silently replaced. One fresh context, one saved submission, no automatic retries, no overwrites or post-save revisions.

DOCUMENT docs/assessment-production/standalone-debates-v1/debate-306/source/assessment-source-notes.md
# Frozen source and assessment restrictions

Motion: Is intelligent design a legitimate scientific explanation for biological origins?

Scored sides: Stephen Meyer (pro); Peter Atkins (con).

By explicit editorial approval, this assessment scores only Stephen Meyer and Peter Atkins in their substantive discussion and both closing remarks, approximately 04:47–1:01:33, excluding programme breaks. This radio discussion has no timed formal rounds and also includes recurring advocate Mark Haville. Haville and host Justin Brierley remain unscored context: their arguments and quoted material are not credited to either scored participant, but Meyer and Atkins’s own replies remain eligible. Unequal prompting and opportunities to respond are considered. Film excerpts, introductions, promotions, Haville’s final promotional remarks, listener feedback and the outro supply no credit, penalty, inferred concession or failure-to-reply inference. The source uses automatic captions and supplementary automated audio checks where available; no direct human listening is claimed.

Event labels E0001–E2090 are one-based; all startEvent/endEvent fields are zero-based. Caption durations overlap speaker turns; a mixed event may contain an unscored tail, never words assignable by proximity alone.

1. The exact motion is Is intelligent design a legitimate scientific explanation for biological origins? Meyer defends a positive inference to intelligence and its scientific legitimacy. Atkins challenges its warrant and status. A critic does not need a completed origin-of-life account merely to question design, although any stronger positive naturalistic claim he actually makes remains assessable.

2. The editor explicitly approved Meyer and Atkins only. Mark Haville is a recurring substantive advocate, not Meyer’s team partner in this assessment. Justin Brierley contributes arguments as well as questions. Both remain context only; their claims, analogies, quotations, demands and rhetoric cannot be credited to either side. Primary speakers’ own replies and explicitly adopted reasoning remain eligible. Consider unequal prompting and opportunities to answer.

3. Assess the four retained programme blocks, including both primary closings. Film trailers and other prerecorded clips, introductions, promotional breaks, Haville’s promotional closing, listener feedback and the outro supply no credit, penalty, inferred concession or missing-reply inference. Full-source access is for scope audit and context, not permission to use excluded evidence.

4. Automatic captions contain overlapping events, mixed turns, misspelled names and corrupted short interjections. Treat disputed scientific and institutional claims as claims made in this debate, not independently verified facts. Do not import external research to repair or refute a speaker’s case, and never turn a recognition defect into a reasoning penalty.

5. Meyer’s opening invokes known causes of information and a historical method he attributes to Darwin. He differentiates functionally specified digital sequence from recurring physical order, allowing energy-driven vortices and other regular structures. Atkins invokes spontaneous structure, thermodynamics and evolution. Evaluate the distinctions actually defended rather than treating complexity, regular order and functional information as interchangeable by fiat.

6. Meyer initially allows change over time while questioning whether chance, law or their combination explain specified sequences. Later he explicitly accepts evolutionary biology as empirical and a valid historical science (about 42:50–43:22). Haville’s preceding denial that macroevolution is empirical belongs to Haville alone. Meyer’s agreement with Atkins must be preserved.

7. Meyer distinguishes information required before the first living/reproducing system from mutation and selection in existing organisms (about 49:13–50:14). He allows modest new information after life exists but questions large transitions. Haville’s preceding absolute assertion that new information cannot be added is not Meyer’s position. Do not collapse origin of life into biological evolution or invent a denial of all mutation-driven information change.

8. Atkins concedes that intelligent-design advocates ask questions scientists should answer, and acknowledges that current science does not yet explain everything. His simplicity/Occam argument is partly methodological and conditional. Do not transform either concession into acceptance of intelligent design, or treat his stated confidence in natural explanation as an already supplied detailed chemical mechanism.

9. On Richard Sternberg, Meyer corrects Brierley’s claim that Sternberg lost his job: Meyer describes removal of office, keys and access, scrutiny and demotion. He invokes peer review and an official report. The film and host’s stronger assertions are not his evidence independently established by this assessment. Preserve his correction and distinguish alleged institutional treatment from truth of design.

10. The Rosetta Stone causal analogy at about 27:40–28:34 is Meyer’s, despite the host initially inviting Mark. Atkins answers that mind is an activity of a material brain; Meyer calls that a materialist presupposition. Do not import Haville’s subsequent social-danger argument or his secondhand quotation about everything from nothing into either scored argument.

11. Meyer distinguishes ordinary operations of nature from questions about origins in response to Atkins’s intelligent-falling analogy. Atkins answers with the interconnectedness of scientific explanations and a preference for simple universal laws. Preserve both the intended domain distinction and the actual coherence challenge; neither alone demonstrates its conclusion by assertion.

12. Meyer contrasts a biological-data inference with biblical deduction. Atkins calls the approach covert creationism and intellectually lazy, identifying divine agency as a premature stopping point. Score the inferential content and actual representation, not tone or politeness alone. Meyer calls God a likely candidate for the inferred agent, not a uniquely established identification.

13. Meyer counters the science-stopper objection through a research agenda, historical religious motivations and the noncoding-DNA prediction; these are distinct kinds of support. Atkins concedes historical motivation but argues science has moved on, and says non-design scientists were already investigating noncoding DNA. Do not assume the claimed prediction was unique, verified or discriminating beyond the reasons actually presented.

14. Atkins’s final remarks defend continuing intelligible explanation and object to a purported limit on understanding; Meyer’s final remarks emphasize freedom of inquiry and room to debate scientific definitions. Atkins speaks after Meyer’s closing, so Meyer’s lack of a later answer is not a concession or automatic response failure.

15. Featured quotes must be exact complete standalone thoughts of the assigned primary speaker, 3–18 words, with necessary qualification preserved. Do not fix caption wording, concatenate separated excerpts, quote mixed-speaker fragments, or shorten a sentence before its meaningful conclusion.

16. The failed evolution-definitions audio input returned no transcription and has not been retried. No independent audio verification is claimed for that interval. Short unresolved overlapping questions around 17:33–18:00 may be context only; they cannot establish a scored claim, concession or contradiction. The underlying information-origin challenge is stated in clear primary-speaker turns elsewhere and need not be inferred from those interruptions.

17. The automated checks used valid primary-speaker references and a Haville reference tied to clear uninterrupted turns. The supplied Justin Brierley reference was subsequently identified as a film-trailer interval and is invalid: disregard all labels based on that reference. Host turns are identified only through named handoffs, programme-management language and dialogue continuity; an unmatched A/B label is not a new participant. No primary ownership may rest solely on an automated label. Preserve this limitation and require below-high confidence wherever material uncertainty remains.

18. The supplemental recognition at about 45:20–45:22 inserts short overlapping words resembling agreement from Atkins, while canonical captions are unclear. Do not infer that Atkins concedes the positive design argument is valid or not an argument from ignorance from this interjection. His sustained questions and objections provide the assessable position.

## Supplementary verification findings

- materialism-and-minds (1590–1817 seconds): The host’s proposed materialist-prejudice symmetry is context. Atkins explicitly acknowledges incomplete present explanations while invoking simplicity. Meyer, despite Mark being invited, makes the Rosetta Stone analogy; Atkins locates mind in a material brain. Haville alone develops the following social-danger and everything-from-nothing rhetoric. Do not use recognition-corrupted short overlap about mind as a concession.

- empirical-and-laziness (2475–2770 seconds): Haville challenges macroevolution as nonempirical; Atkins cites paleobiological evidence. Meyer explicitly agrees that evolutionary biology is empirical, then invokes historical scientific reasoning for design. Atkins’s inquiry-stopper/laziness challenge is his own, while the host’s formulation remains context. Haville makes the atheistic-gaps interjection. Meyer defends a positive known-cause inference and calls God a likely candidate. A tiny overlapping apparent Atkins agreement in supplemental recognition is not reliable concession evidence.

- information-and-origin (2805–3050 seconds): Haville raises Max Planck; Meyer separately adds Maxwell, but the general speech about modern religious scientists is Haville’s. Haville states that adding new information cannot happen; Atkins answers with copying mistakes, survival and propagation. Meyer then distinguishes origins before life from changes after life, allows modest information gains by mutation, questions major transitions, and says he would accept a demonstrated natural origin-of-life explanation. Preserve those qualifications and do not attribute Haville’s absolute claim to Meyer.

- inquiry-and-predictions (3050–3295 seconds): The host challenges laziness first; Meyer asks about assuming material processes regardless of evidence. Atkins distinguishes simple underlying ideas from complex outcomes and explicitly says design is unnecessary. Meyer advances the research/prediction example about noncoding DNA. Atkins answers that non-design scientists already investigated it. Haville asks that other researchers be allowed to answer; Atkins supplies the schoolyard dismissal. Only the primary speakers’ own arguments are eligible.

- both-primary-closings (3470–3697 seconds): Both closings are present. Meyer emphasizes freedom of inquiry, proper credentials and debate about scientific definitions; Atkins emphasizes no demonstrated need for design, premature agency answers and continuing human understanding. The host’s intervening framing is context. Atkins ends last; no later Meyer response opportunity exists.

No scoring evidence may depend on the failed first check, the invalid host reference, an uncertain tiny interjection, or a third participant’s words. If a selected material uncertainty still remains, report below-high confidence and request the required pre-adjudication verification; do not conceal uncertainty to avoid the gate.

END DOCUMENT
